# 309: Chapter 310: As Expected

Incineroar—there was no need to say much. Dylan had one, Selene had one, Kukui had one… and now Kieran had one too.

Not to mention the wave of Trainers who, ever since Dylan's Incineroar became famous, flocked to Alola just to find a Litten of their own.

As soon as Incineroar entered the field, it let out a low, threatening growl at Frosmoth. An aura of dominance radiated from it.

Intimidate!

A white light shimmered around Frosmoth—it had its physical attack lowered by one stage. Not that it mattered much. Frosmoth was a special attacker through and through.

"Bug Buzz!"

Dylan narrowed his eyes slightly. If he remembered right, Kieran's Incineroar knew Fake Out.

Frosmoth was a poor match into Incineroar, being 4x weak to Fire. Honestly, it was lucky Incineroar was also part Dark, otherwise it would resist Bug moves too.

Frosmoth's wings started to hum rapidly, preparing the Bug-type attack.

"Fake Out!" Kieran called, just as expected.

Incineroar lunged forward, its broad form slicing through the snowy battlefield like a red streak.

Priority move: Fake Out!

In a blink, it was beneath Frosmoth—its paw slammed upward. Frosmoth's shimmering Aurora Veil pulsed.

Smack!

The attack landed clean. Frosmoth gave a sharp cry, flinching back—the Fake Out had done its job.

"Flamethrower!" Kieran didn't hesitate.

He knew the combo well. If Incineroar weren't falling from the air at that moment, he might've gone with Flare Blitz for maximum impact.

As Incineroar descended, flames erupted from its jaws. A blazing pillar tore through the snow, crashing into Frosmoth.

The Aurora Veil shimmered again, halving the damage. And thanks to its Ice Scales ability, Frosmoth took even less from special attacks.

Combined with its decent Special Defense, the hit was manageable.

"Aurora Veil's damage reduction is insane, and then there's Ice Scales on top…" Carmine frowned. That combo had neutered what should've been a devastating follow-up.

"Not to mention, Mr. Dylan's counterattack is coming," Lacey noted, eyes flicking toward the dying fire.

"Bug Buzz!" Dylan commanded.

Frosmoth forced itself to endure the flames. Its majestic snowflake wings pulsed—bright rings of sound exploded outward, shattering the lingering blaze.

BZZZZZ—!

The wave of sound barreled toward Incineroar, who had just landed.

A swift counter.

"Fire Punch!"

Incineroar roared, its paw bursting into flames as it punched toward the oncoming sound waves.

Boom!

Fire met sound in mid-air, but Incineroar's face twisted. It had forced the punch—but the Bug Buzz was too powerful.

Flames shattered, and the sonic wave slammed into Incineroar, making it growl in pain.

"That special attack power is no joke…" Kieran looked at the elegant moth.

This icy beauty's Sp. Atk stat had to be on par with Volcarona. Dylan had trained it extremely well.

"The snow's about to end." Kieran glanced upward. The snowy clouds were thinning. No snow meant Aurora Veil would vanish soon.

"Close in—Bug Buzz," Dylan said calmly.

Frosmoth lowered slightly, wings humming again. The sound waves shimmered like ripples toward Incineroar.

"Flame Charge!"

Kieran gave the order. Fire-type Flame Charge—a move that raised Speed every time it landed.

Incineroar burst into flames and charged through the snow, trying to avoid the attack—but it wasn't fast enough yet.

BOOM!

The Bug Buzz caught up, slamming into Incineroar's back. The big cat roared in pain, its body sent flying.

That Bug Buzz had been even faster than before!

Kieran was sure of it—no way Incineroar should've been hit otherwise.

Above, the clouds finally broke. The snow ended.

"Another Bug Buzz!"

"Protect!"

There was no escaping—Incineroar had just been hit. It couldn't dodge.

Waves of sound surged forward. Incineroar braced, forming a green shield just in time.

The sound exploded against the barrier but couldn't break through.

"Flare Blitz!"

"Dazzling Gleam!"

As the shield faded, Incineroar let out a furious roar, igniting in red-hot flames.

Flare Blitz—a reckless, full-power Fire move.

It launched toward the airborne Frosmoth with all its might.

Frosmoth rose higher, pink light flickering around its body.

Fairy-type Dazzling Gleam—a beautiful beam of light shot toward Incineroar.

But Flare Blitz tore through it. Incineroar's brute strength burned right through the beam.

Still… it couldn't fly. And it wasn't fast enough.

Frosmoth outpaced it easily with its wings.

"Incineroar's nearly spent…" Lillie murmured. The tiger's speed was dropping—it was already starting to fall.

"Bug Buzz." Dylan's eyes glinted.

He glanced briefly at Kieran's focused expression, then gave the order.

He wasn't too keen on striking right now—Incineroar could still retaliate. But hey, it was a Gym battle, not a deathmatch. A little mercy wouldn't hurt.

Frosmoth descended a bit, hovering just above Incineroar's range. It hummed again, sending another wave of sound pulsing outward.

"Fire Blast!"

Kieran was waiting for this.

Incineroar roared. Flames erupted from its belt—forming the shape of a massive kanji character for "big." Fire Blast!

BOOM!

Frosmoth's Bug Buzz landed, sending Incineroar tumbling—but the Fire Blast struck back.

Frosmoth's wings glimmered—it activated Ice Scales, halving special damage.

A move that should've been 4x super-effective instead only hit for 2x—and Frosmoth, while clearly injured, held on.

Kieran's frown deepened. That wasn't worth it.

Incineroar hit the ground, its breath labored.

It had taken three Bug Buzzes. Even though they weren't super effective, Frosmoth's raw power was too high.

"Return, Incineroar."

Kieran hesitated, then recalled the tiger. Intimidate was too valuable to waste. He didn't want to lose it here.

He couldn't afford for Frosmoth to take it down.

"Grimmsnarl!"

Kieran tossed his third Poké Ball.

A hulking, hairy Pokémon landed in front of him—its body covered in long fur, with sharp fangs and pointed ears. Grimmsnarl.

Bzzt~ Grimmsnarl, Dark/Fairy type, the Bulk Up Pokémon.

Ability: Prankster

Moves: Hammer Arm, Foul Play, Play Rough, Nasty Plot, Spirit Break, Fake Out, Sucker Punch…

"Grimmsnarl, huh…" Leaf eyed Kieran curiously. Despite his clean appearance, his team choices were... bold.

Incineroar and Grimmsnarl? Very "bro energy."

As expected from a boy Dylan personally believed in. Plenty of promise.

"Grimmsnarl? Not bad," Dylan said, giving the Pokémon a once-over. Solid choice.

"Thank you for the praise, Mr. Dylan. Now, let's keep going!"

Kieran grinned—clearly pleased to be acknowledged, but focused on the battle.

"Grimmsnarl—Fake Out!"

The goblin snarled and rushed forward. Frosmoth tried to retreat, but Grimmsnarl was too fast.

Its claws slammed down—Fake Out connected clean.

Frosmoth cried out and flinched, driven back.

"Now—Burning Jealousy!"

Grimmsnarl descended, its body flaring with flames before blasting a fiery shockwave toward Frosmoth.

Frosmoth's Ice Scales shimmered, but the hit was still powerful.

It was thrown back, crashing into the ground.

Its icy wings glistened… but its eyes had turned into spirals.

"Frosmoth is unable to battle. Grimmsnarl wins," Leaf declared from the field.

From Politoed, to Incineroar, to Grimmsnarl—it had taken three to do it, but Kieran had finally taken down Frosmoth.

He exhaled, a smile spreading on his face.

"Nice one, Kieran! First win secured!" Carmine pumped her fist, cheering her brother on.

"True. Cost him a bit, but taking down one of Dylan's team is still big," Lacey said with a smile—though deep down, she knew Kieran didn't hold the advantage yet.

Politoed had been frozen, Incineroar heavily wounded… and that had only been Dylan's first main attacker.

On the field.

"Well done, Kieran. That was a solid takedown," Dylan said, withdrawing Frosmoth. His tone remained gentle, full of praise.

He quickly sent out another ball.

Alolan Ninetales appeared again, its cry ringing through the field.

Snow began to fall once more.

"Will he use Aurora Veil again?" Kieran eyed the stunning fox.

He couldn't stop the weather change—his Politoed was still frozen. But would Dylan go for the screen again?

"Baton Pass."

"Grimmsnarl—Bulk Up!"

Kieran didn't waste time. He couldn't stop Ninetales's retreat, but he could at least use the opportunity to set up.

Grimmsnarl roared, striking a few flexing poses. Its body swelled with power.

Tch… Dylan chuckled inwardly. Smart plays, as always.

Ninetales vanished in white light. Dylan tossed another Ultra Ball.

"Then Kieran—your next opponent… is this one."

He tossed his fourth Pokémon.

"Weavile, ready for battle!"

(End of Chapter)

---

---

---

# 310: Chapter 311: It’s So Strong

Kieran carefully considered which Pokémon to send out next against Weavile. No matter what, he had to defeat this Weavile first.

Mr. Dylan had said he would use Ogerpon in the battle, but at this rate, if he couldn't get past Weavile, he wouldn't even have a chance to see Ogerpon.

Kieran really liked Ogerpon. Although Mr. Dylan had already captured it, at the very least, Kieran wanted to witness its power in battle.

"Porygon-Z!"

Kieran finally made his decision and threw out his fourth Pokémon.

A flash of white light, and a Pokémon that looked like a toy bird appeared in front of Kieran.

Its body was red and blue, with its head detached from its torso—this was Porygon's final evolution: Porygon-Z.

'Beep~ Porygon-Z. Normal-type. Virtual Pokémon.

Ability: Adaptability

Moves: Hyper Beam, Zap Cannon, Ice Beam, Tri Attack, Discharge, Recover…'

Description: Programs were added to make it a better Pokémon, but for some reason, its behavior became increasingly erratic.

This Porygon-Z looked quite promising.

Lillie glanced at Kieran and suddenly realized—didn't her brother have all the same Pokémon as Kieran?

The Politoed, Incineroar, Grimmsnarl, and now this Porygon-Z—her brother had raised all of them too.

Emmm... well, that made sense. Her brother had so many Pokémon.

But it also showed that Kieran had great taste; every Pokémon he trained was outstanding.

"Quick Attack."

Dylan took one look at the Porygon-Z and took the initiative to strike.

"Trick Room!"

Kieran shouted decisively.

Porygon-Z raised its head, and a spatial cube began spinning and forming in front of it—Trick Room.

Bang!

Weavile slammed into Porygon-Z with Quick Attack, making its body shudder.

"Focus Blast!"

The Trick Room expanded rapidly to cover the entire battlefield, warping the space around them.

Weavile's right claw glowed blue—Focus Blast, a Fighting-type move!

Boom!

Focus Blast struck Porygon-Z in the chest, sending it flying with a cry of pain.

Focus Blast, being Fighting-type, was super effective against Porygon-Z. It dealt massive damage.

"Return, Porygon-Z." Kieran immediately recalled Porygon-Z with its Poké Ball.

"So that's it—using Trick Room? That way, Weavile's high speed actually becomes a disadvantage."

Lillie looked at the battlefield now cloaked in strange space. She had thought Kieran planned to paralyze Weavile with Thunder Wave once it got close.

Paralysis would've drastically reduced Weavile's speed and caused occasional inability to act—but Trick Room worked just as well.

Trick Room, a Psychic-type move, created a bizarre space in which slower Pokémon moved first while faster ones were hindered.

Such was the strange power of this move.

"Hydrapple! You're up!"

Kieran tossed out his fifth Poké Ball, and a giant apple thudded to the ground in front of him.

Then a thick, green, wormlike body rose from the apple, and six smaller "apple worms" emerged, hissing at Weavile before quickly retreating back inside.

This was Hydrapple, the final evolution of Applin!

'Beep~ Hydrapple. Grass/Dragon-type. Apple Hydra Pokémon

Ability: Sweet Nectar

Moves: Dragon Pulse, Power Whip, Substitute, Energy Ball, Recover, Syrup Bomb, Tera Burst...

Description: Formed by the gathering of moody Applins. When they are of one mind, they unleash their true power.'

"This thing's actually a Dragon-type…"

Lan couldn't help but comment after reading the Pokédex. Dragon-types sure were full of bizarre creatures—this one even had "apple" in its name.

"But Hydrapple being Grass and Dragon means it's 4x weak to Ice."

Lillie looked puzzled—why would Kieran use Hydrapple against Weavile?

Even if Trick Room had reversed their speed, Hydrapple was still 4x weak to Ice-type moves.

"Hydrapple is Kieran's ace Pokémon—it's incredibly powerful. Don't forget about Terastallization, Lillie."

Lacey reminded her. Hydrapple might have a major Ice weakness, but Terastallization could change that.

"Oh, right." Lillie blinked, then remembered.

Blueberry Academy was all about Terastallization, and since Kieran was now their strongest trainer, he surely had mastered it.

"Mr. Dylan, this is my strongest Pokémon," Kieran said solemnly to Dylan. Hydrapple was undoubtedly his trump card.

"Please bear witness!"

Kieran pulled out a black Tera Orb, which began glowing brightly. He hurled it at Hydrapple with force.

Terastallize!

Hydrapple's entire body glowed brilliantly, shimmering like a crystal gemstone.

With the sound of shattering crystal, Terastallized Hydrapple emerged, glowing and adorned with the Fighting Tera symbol above its head.

"A Fighting Tera Type, huh."

Dylan examined this Terastallized Hydrapple—it had the same Tera Type as in Kieran's game version.

With Fighting Tera, it now countered Ice-types and was no longer weak to them.

That's what made Terastallization so powerful—it reversed type disadvantages, especially useful for Pokémon like Hydrapple with 4x weaknesses.

"Hydrapple, charge!"

Kieran gave the command without hesitation.

Trick Room had a time limit—he had to defeat Weavile before it ended.

Hydrapple hissed as it dragged its apple forward at surprising speed under the effects of Trick Room.

"Dragon Pulse!"

Hydrapple opened its mouth wide, and several smaller "apple worms" emerged, charging up energy.

Dragon-type Dragon Pulse! When multiple heads fired together, the beam's power doubled!

Several pulses merged into one thick laser that shot toward Weavile.

Weavile leapt backward to dodge, but under Trick Room, its normally agile body now felt sluggish and heavy.

Boom!

The powerful Dragon Pulse struck Weavile's previous position, exploding violently, and the shockwave still hit Weavile.

Weavile grunted, forced into another retreat—but as it looked up again, Hydrapple was already right in front of it!

"Icicle Crash!" Dylan raised an eyebrow at the sight.

Weavile slashed its right claw, summoning giant icicles from the air and launching them at Hydrapple.

"Pollen Puff!" Kieran countered.

Hydrapple roared and fired a green energy sphere toward Weavile.

Bug-type Pollen Puff!

Boom!

The icicles slammed into Hydrapple, making it cry out in pain.

But at the same time, Pollen Puff hit Weavile squarely, exploding and sending it flying—Bug-type moves were super effective against Weavile, and the damage was high.

"Tera Burst!" Kieran ordered a follow-up strike.

Icicle Crash didn't counter a now Fighting-type Hydrapple, and with its solid defense, Hydrapple had taken the hit well.

Hydrapple charged its mouth with Tera energy, forming a massive energy fist and fired it at Weavile—Fighting-type Tera Burst!

"Protect."

Weavile couldn't take a 4x-effective Tera Burst. It would be a one-hit KO.

Gritting its teeth, Weavile raised a protective shield.

Boom!

Tera Burst smashed into the shield and exploded, but it didn't break through.

"Dragon Pulse!" Hydrapple surged forward again, gathering energy in its mouth.

It was waiting for Protect to wear off.

"Quick Attack!"

As soon as Protect faded, Weavile bolted away, dodging Dragon Pulse.

"Don't rush."

Kieran stayed calm. Even with Trick Room making Hydrapple faster, Weavile's Quick Attack still gave it great mobility.

Bang!

Weavile suddenly appeared behind Hydrapple and slammed into it.

"Tera Burst!"

"Aerial Ace!"

Hydrapple whipped around, energy building once more.

But Weavile leapt up and spun around, cloaked in white wind energy.

Flying-type Aerial Ace!

Pow!

The strike hit Hydrapple's head, making it cry out. Flying was strong against Fighting—this hit was solid.

But a moment later, Tera Burst launched, smashing straight into Weavile.

Weavile was blasted away by the exploding energy fist, crashing hard into the ground.

"Weavile is unable to battle. Hydrapple wins," Lan declared.

"Well done, Kieran!" Carmine was thrilled for her brother.

A 4x-effective Tera Burst—there was no way Weavile could survive that. Tera Fighting was a brilliant move.

"Nicely done. That Trick Room was clever."

Dylan recalled Weavile and clapped for Kieran.

Trick Room was a very niche move. Few used it, as it only altered speed for a short time and didn't directly raise power.

But often, battles were lost simply due to lack of speed.

Without Trick Room shifting the speed dynamic, Hydrapple would've had a hard time ever landing a hit on Weavile.

Just like Grimmsnarl earlier—beaten by Weavile's speed and agility.

"Kieran, you've really grown strong."

Dylan pulled out a crimson Luxury Ball, genuinely impressed—not just by Kieran's strength, but by his tactical mind. Coming up with Trick Room to counter Weavile mid-battle showed sharp awareness.

"Then next up, just as we promised."

The Luxury Ball in his hand expanded.

"Kieran, are you ready?"

"Yes, Mr. Dylan!"

Kieran's eyes glowed with excitement, though he kept his emotions in check and responded solemnly.

That long-passed-down "ghost"!

"Very good, then…" Dylan chuckled softly and threw out his fifth Pokémon.

"Ogerpon! Prepare for battle!"

# 311: Chapter 312: This Thing’s Actually a Dragon-Type Pokémon

Kieran carefully considered which Pokémon to send out next against Weavile. No matter what, he had to defeat this Weavile first.

Mr. Dylan had said he would use Ogerpon in the battle, but at this rate, if he couldn't get past Weavile, he wouldn't even have a chance to see Ogerpon.

Kieran really liked Ogerpon. Although Mr. Dylan had already captured it, at the very least, Kieran wanted to witness its power in battle.

"Porygon-Z!"

Kieran finally made his decision and threw out his fourth Pokémon.

A flash of white light, and a Pokémon that looked like a toy bird appeared in front of Kieran.

Its body was red and blue, with its head detached from its torso—this was Porygon's final evolution: Porygon-Z.

'Beep~ Porygon-Z. Normal-type. Virtual Pokémon.

Ability: Adaptability

Moves: Hyper Beam, Zap Cannon, Ice Beam, Tri Attack, Discharge, Recover…'

Description: Programs were added to make it a better Pokémon, but for some reason, its behavior became increasingly erratic.

This Porygon-Z looked quite promising.

Lillie glanced at Kieran and suddenly realized—didn't her brother have all the same Pokémon as Kieran?

The Politoed, Incineroar, Grimmsnarl, and now this Porygon-Z—her brother had raised all of them too.

Emmm... well, that made sense. Her brother had so many Pokémon.

But it also showed that Kieran had great taste; every Pokémon he trained was outstanding.

"Quick Attack."

Dylan took one look at the Porygon-Z and took the initiative to strike.

"Trick Room!"

Kieran shouted decisively.

Porygon-Z raised its head, and a spatial cube began spinning and forming in front of it—Trick Room.

Bang!

Weavile slammed into Porygon-Z with Quick Attack, making its body shudder.

"Focus Blast!"

The Trick Room expanded rapidly to cover the entire battlefield, warping the space around them.

Weavile's right claw glowed blue—Focus Blast, a Fighting-type move!

Boom!

Focus Blast struck Porygon-Z in the chest, sending it flying with a cry of pain.

Focus Blast, being Fighting-type, was super effective against Porygon-Z. It dealt massive damage.

"Return, Porygon-Z." Kieran immediately recalled Porygon-Z with its Poké Ball.

"So that's it—using Trick Room? That way, Weavile's high speed actually becomes a disadvantage."

Lillie looked at the battlefield now cloaked in strange space. She had thought Kieran planned to paralyze Weavile with Thunder Wave once it got close.

Paralysis would've drastically reduced Weavile's speed and caused occasional inability to act—but Trick Room worked just as well.

Trick Room, a Psychic-type move, created a bizarre space in which slower Pokémon moved first while faster ones were hindered.

Such was the strange power of this move.

"Hydrapple! You're up!"

Kieran tossed out his fifth Poké Ball, and a giant apple thudded to the ground in front of him.

Then a thick, green, wormlike body rose from the apple, and six smaller "apple worms" emerged, hissing at Weavile before quickly retreating back inside.

This was Hydrapple, the final evolution of Applin!

'Beep~ Hydrapple. Grass/Dragon-type. Apple Hydra Pokémon

Ability: Sweet Nectar

Moves: Dragon Pulse, Power Whip, Substitute, Energy Ball, Recover, Syrup Bomb, Tera Burst...

Description: Formed by the gathering of moody Applins. When they are of one mind, they unleash their true power.'

"This thing's actually a Dragon-type…"

Lan couldn't help but comment after reading the Pokédex. Dragon-types sure were full of bizarre creatures—this one even had "apple" in its name.

"But Hydrapple being Grass and Dragon means it's 4x weak to Ice."

Lillie looked puzzled—why would Kieran use Hydrapple against Weavile?

Even if Trick Room had reversed their speed, Hydrapple was still 4x weak to Ice-type moves.

"Hydrapple is Kieran's ace Pokémon—it's incredibly powerful. Don't forget about Terastallization, Lillie."

Lacey reminded her. Hydrapple might have a major Ice weakness, but Terastallization could change that.

"Oh, right." Lillie blinked, then remembered.

Blueberry Academy was all about Terastallization, and since Kieran was now their strongest trainer, he surely had mastered it.

"Mr. Dylan, this is my strongest Pokémon," Kieran said solemnly to Dylan. Hydrapple was undoubtedly his trump card.

"Please bear witness!"

Kieran pulled out a black Tera Orb, which began glowing brightly. He hurled it at Hydrapple with force.

Terastallize!

Hydrapple's entire body glowed brilliantly, shimmering like a crystal gemstone.

With the sound of shattering crystal, Terastallized Hydrapple emerged, glowing and adorned with the Fighting Tera symbol above its head.

"A Fighting Tera Type, huh."

Dylan examined this Terastallized Hydrapple—it had the same Tera Type as in Kieran's game version.

With Fighting Tera, it now countered Ice-types and was no longer weak to them.

That's what made Terastallization so powerful—it reversed type disadvantages, especially useful for Pokémon like Hydrapple with 4x weaknesses.

"Hydrapple, charge!"

Kieran gave the command without hesitation.

Trick Room had a time limit—he had to defeat Weavile before it ended.

Hydrapple hissed as it dragged its apple forward at surprising speed under the effects of Trick Room.

"Dragon Pulse!"

Hydrapple opened its mouth wide, and several smaller "apple worms" emerged, charging up energy.

Dragon-type Dragon Pulse! When multiple heads fired together, the beam's power doubled!

Several pulses merged into one thick laser that shot toward Weavile.

Weavile leapt backward to dodge, but under Trick Room, its normally agile body now felt sluggish and heavy.

Boom!

The powerful Dragon Pulse struck Weavile's previous position, exploding violently, and the shockwave still hit Weavile.

Weavile grunted, forced into another retreat—but as it looked up again, Hydrapple was already right in front of it!

"Icicle Crash!" Dylan raised an eyebrow at the sight.

Weavile slashed its right claw, summoning giant icicles from the air and launching them at Hydrapple.

"Pollen Puff!" Kieran countered.

Hydrapple roared and fired a green energy sphere toward Weavile.

Bug-type Pollen Puff!

Boom!

The icicles slammed into Hydrapple, making it cry out in pain.

But at the same time, Pollen Puff hit Weavile squarely, exploding and sending it flying—Bug-type moves were super effective against Weavile, and the damage was high.

"Tera Burst!" Kieran ordered a follow-up strike.

Icicle Crash didn't counter a now Fighting-type Hydrapple, and with its solid defense, Hydrapple had taken the hit well.

Hydrapple charged its mouth with Tera energy, forming a massive energy fist and fired it at Weavile—Fighting-type Tera Burst!

"Protect."

Weavile couldn't take a 4x-effective Tera Burst. It would be a one-hit KO.

Gritting its teeth, Weavile raised a protective shield.

Boom!

Tera Burst smashed into the shield and exploded, but it didn't break through.

"Dragon Pulse!" Hydrapple surged forward again, gathering energy in its mouth.

It was waiting for Protect to wear off.

"Quick Attack!"

As soon as Protect faded, Weavile bolted away, dodging Dragon Pulse.

"Don't rush."

Kieran stayed calm. Even with Trick Room making Hydrapple faster, Weavile's Quick Attack still gave it great mobility.

Bang!

Weavile suddenly appeared behind Hydrapple and slammed into it.

"Tera Burst!"

"Aerial Ace!"

Hydrapple whipped around, energy building once more.

But Weavile leapt up and spun around, cloaked in white wind energy.

Flying-type Aerial Ace!

Pow!

The strike hit Hydrapple's head, making it cry out. Flying was strong against Fighting—this hit was solid.

But a moment later, Tera Burst launched, smashing straight into Weavile.

Weavile was blasted away by the exploding energy fist, crashing hard into the ground.

"Weavile is unable to battle. Hydrapple wins," Lan declared.

"Well done, Kieran!" Carmine was thrilled for her brother.

A 4x-effective Tera Burst—there was no way Weavile could survive that. Tera Fighting was a brilliant move.

"Nicely done. That Trick Room was clever."

Dylan recalled Weavile and clapped for Kieran.

Trick Room was a very niche move. Few used it, as it only altered speed for a short time and didn't directly raise power.

But often, battles were lost simply due to lack of speed.

Without Trick Room shifting the speed dynamic, Hydrapple would've had a hard time ever landing a hit on Weavile.

Just like Grimmsnarl earlier—beaten by Weavile's speed and agility.

"Kieran, you've really grown strong."

Dylan pulled out a crimson Luxury Ball, genuinely impressed—not just by Kieran's strength, but by his tactical mind. Coming up with Trick Room to counter Weavile mid-battle showed sharp awareness.

"Then next up, just as we promised."

The Luxury Ball in his hand expanded.

"Kieran, are you ready?"

"Yes, Mr. Dylan!"

Kieran's eyes glowed with excitement, though he kept his emotions in check and responded solemnly.

That long-passed-down "oni"!

"Very good, then…" Dylan chuckled softly and threw out his fifth Pokémon.

"Ogerpon! Prepare for battle!"

# 312: Chapter 313: You Should Go to Alola

"Ogerpon, prepare for battle!"

"Ogy~"

The Luxury Ball opened, and Ogerpon landed in front of Dylan, taking out the Teal Mask and placing it on her face.

"Ogerpon!"

Kieran instinctively clenched his fists, excitement and anticipation lighting up his eyes as he stared at Ogerpon.

The legendary "evil spirit" of Kitakami, the one said to have defeated the Loyal Three in ancient times.

Although the villagers of Mossui Town feared and shunned this "ghost," Kieran was different. He admired the power it represented.

Unfortunately, Ogerpon had been captured by Mr. Dylan. Still, Kieran didn't mind. Dylan was so powerful—Kieran might envy him, but he didn't feel jealous.

And now, he was finally going to experience Ogerpon's strength firsthand!

"Mr. Dylan, please don't hold back!" Kieran said seriously.

"Oh? I see." Dylan paused, then smiled in understanding.

"Then let's go all out, Ogy."

"Ogy~!"

Ogerpon let out a low cry. To the astonishment of Carmine and the others, her entire body began to shine with dazzling crystal light—Terastallization!

"What?" Carmine gasped, covering her mouth in shock.

She wasn't surprised that Ogerpon could Terastallize—after all, any Pokémon could do that.

What stunned her was that Dylan hadn't used a Tera Orb at all!

"Auto-Terastallization?" Lacey blinked her big eyes.

"That's right. Ogerpon doesn't need my brother to use a Tera Orb—she can Terastallize on her own," Lillie said, her smile radiant.

The Terastallization didn't last long. With a crisp sound of crystal shattering, a massive, ornate, and radiant mask appeared. Behind it stood Ogerpon's body, glowing with Tera light.

Bathed in the mask's brilliance, Ogerpon's speed increased!

"This is a unique Terastallization exclusive to Ogy. Kieran, come—let me see how you plan to deal with it."

Dylan raised his arm and gestured for Kieran to make his move.

"Hydrapple! Dragon Pulse!"

Kieran's chest rose and fell rapidly, his emotions surging. But he forced himself to stay calm and called out loudly to Hydrapple.

Hydrapple roared. Several Applins emerged from the apple, combining with the main body to build up energy. The enhanced Dragon Pulse shot toward Ogerpon at high speed.

"Barbed Vine Staff," Dylan said with a faint smile.

Ogerpon raised her right hand, and a thorny staff entwined with green vines appeared. With a sweep of her left arm, the massive Teal Mask shifted aside.

Boom!

Ogerpon brought the vine staff down hard on the incoming Dragon Pulse, unleashing a thunderous explosion.

The Dragon Pulse was completely shattered by the vine staff, its scattered energy dispersing in a violent shockwave.

"Quick Attack."

A flash of white light burst behind Ogerpon—she shot toward Hydrapple like lightning.

Trick Room was nearly over, but still in effect, so Ogerpon had to use a priority move like Quick Attack to close the gap.

"Hydrapple!"

Bang!

Before Kieran could finish his warning, Ogerpon had already slammed into Hydrapple.

"Play Rough!"

"Pollen Puff!"

Pink Fairy energy wrapped around Ogerpon's limbs as she pounced on Hydrapple and launched a relentless assault—Fairy-type Play Rough!

Under the barrage, Hydrapple howled in pain. The Pollen Puff gathering in its mouth completely dissipated.

Bang!

With one final punch, Ogerpon sent Hydrapple flying. It crashed heavily to the ground.

When they looked again, Hydrapple's main body—the Applin—was clearly unable to battle, and its Terastallization faded.

Fighting Tera Hydrapple was weak to Fairy. That Play Rough from Ogerpon was too much for it to handle.

"Hydrapple is unable to battle. Ogerpon wins!"

Lan announced the result.

The match had ended almost shockingly fast.

"So strong…"

Carmine stared in disbelief at the crystal-clear Ogerpon. So this was the power of the legendary "ghost"?

Kieran's ace Hydrapple had been defeated so easily.

"Ogy's power is second only to a certain tier. Among my brother's main team, she's still at the top," Lillie said without surprise.

Aside from the highest-level legends, Ogerpon was Dylan's strongest Pokémon—without a doubt.

Even Darkrai or Zapdos couldn't compare.

That tier... which tier exactly?

Carmine wanted to ask but decided not to.

Lacey, however, seemed to understand. She remembered those Pokémon that once made her Brute Bonnet tremble—and went quiet.

On the battlefield—

After defeating Hydrapple, Ogerpon waved her arm. The crystal Teal Mask floated back in front of her, pulsing like a living thing.

"Return, Hydrapple. You did great."

Even though Hydrapple had been defeated easily, Kieran didn't blame it. He took a deep breath.

"I expected this, but still… it's really strong."

Kieran stared at that glowing, green evil mask and took out another Poké Ball.

"Porygon-Z!"

He sent out Porygon-Z once again.

By now, the strange spatial distortion on the field had ended—Trick Room was over.

"Ice Beam."

Kieran immediately gave the order.

Porygon-Z began charging a pale blue energy orb in front of it.

"Power Whip."

Ogerpon stomped the ground and growled. Vines burst from beneath and wove into a massive whip, lashing downward at Porygon-Z.

Ice Beam clashed with the whip, but only for a moment—Power Whip shattered the beam and kept going.

Boom!

The massive vine struck Porygon-Z hard, slamming it into the battlefield.

The entire ground trembled violently, dust and debris billowing up.

Kieran's heart skipped. His Porygon-Z had incredible special attack, but it had been overpowered just like that…

Ogerpon waved her hand, and the crystal Teal Mask stirred up a strong gust that blew the dust away.

"Porygon-Z is unable to battle. Ogerpon wins."

Lan announced again.

"They're not even on the same level."

Lacey let out a sigh. From that exchange alone, it was clear—there was a massive gap in strength.

"Even if Ogerpon had appeared back then, Kieran wouldn't have been able to capture her," Carmine said quietly after a pause.

The stories from Kitakami had always suggested Ogerpon was powerful—but no one imagined she was this overwhelming.

"Emmm… that's true," Lillie said thoughtfully, tapping her chin. "Even though Ogy hadn't been caught by my brother back then, if today's Kieran had tried to catch her back then, he still wouldn't stand a chance."

She remembered it vividly—Ogerpon entering Terastallization on her own and obliterating Peachy and the others in a one-vs-four like a war god.

Her power had been staggering.

Lacey: (—)

Carmine: "..."

Back on the battlefield, Kieran still hadn't given up—even though the gap in power was now painfully clear.

But this was his promise with Dylan. If they hadn't made that agreement, he might not even have gotten the chance to battle Ogerpon.

Even if he couldn't win, Kieran wouldn't surrender.

His sixth Pokémon was Dragonite—but it didn't make a difference. Ogerpon's single Play Rough took Dragonite down instantly.

And as for the frozen Politoed? That was even worse. Ogerpon used Power Whip once—the ice broke, sure, but Politoed ended up with spinning eyes.

"Politoed is unable to battle. Ogerpon wins. Challenger Kieran's six Pokémon are all unable to battle. The challenge is over."

Lan declared the final result.

"Phew~"

Kieran recalled Politoed and let out a long sigh. Looking at the Poké Ball in his hand, he felt a bit down—but also strangely at peace.

"Well done, Kieran."

A gentle voice brought him back to his senses.

"Mr. Dylan."

Kieran looked up to see Dylan approaching, Ogerpon beside him, her Terastallization now gone.

"That was a great battle. I've said it before, but I'll say it again—you've really grown. You're a true man now."

Dylan patted Kieran's shoulder with a warm smile.

"Thank you, Mr. Dylan! It's all thanks to your encouragement back then!" Kieran bit his lip, then stepped back and bowed respectfully.

"No, Kieran—the most important thing is your own heart."

Dylan chuckled softly.

"You had the will to persevere and grow. You unlocked your own potential. You've always been outstanding—I merely gave a bit of guidance."

"If you didn't already have the talent, you wouldn't have come this far."

Dylan looked at Kieran as he spoke. Yes, it might sound harsh, but it was the truth.

Kieran had become a Champion-level trainer in such a short time because of his talent and potential. For someone of average talent, it simply wouldn't have been possible.

"Without you, Mr. Dylan, I might still be that timid Kieran." But Kieran shook his head.

He knew his strength came from his own hard training, but without the determination to grow stronger, he wouldn't have come so far.

"Then I'll accept your thanks—and give you this."

Dylan didn't argue further. He took out a small ornate box and opened it in front of Kieran.

"This is the Ice-type Rota Badge. Kieran, you've earned it."

Kieran's eyes lit up at the sight of the badge.

It was carved from icy blue gemstone in the shape of a snowflake, delicate and beautiful, almost glowing faintly.

"Wow! It's so pretty!" Lacey came over and gasped at the badge's beauty.

That snowflake design really was stunning.

"Mr. Dylan, I…" Kieran had instantly fallen in love with the badge, but he still hesitated.

"Don't refuse. Take it—you deserve it. It's my recognition of you," Dylan said with a smile, placing the box in Kieran's hands.

"Take it, Kieran. Mr. Dylan doesn't give out badges lightly," Lacey urged him as well.

"All right… Thank you, Mr. Dylan." Kieran bowed again with sincere gratitude.

"You earned my approval. No thanks necessary," Dylan shook his head.

A simple 'thank you' wouldn't earn his badge. Kieran truly had what it took.

The battle was over. Dylan gave the three of them a quick tour of the Rota Gym before bringing them to the lounge.

"The year's almost over—soon it'll be a new year. Kieran, Miss Lacey, do you have any plans for next year?" Dylan asked, sipping tea.

"I haven't graduated yet. But I plan to travel around Unova for a bit and try challenging the Unova League."

Lacey tilted her head, thinking it over. She was currently a second-year at Blueberry Academy. Next year, she'd be a third-year.

But staying at school too long didn't feel right. There were strong classmates to battle, sure—but not enough.

She wasn't as gifted as Kieran, after all.

"Traveling is a good choice. You'll gain perspective, experience, and new partners."

Dylan fully supported the idea.

"What about you, Kieran?"

"I'm… not sure yet," Kieran replied after a moment.

He truly didn't know what to do next year. He was already Champion-level—staying at Blueberry Academy wasn't an option anymore.

After all, it was just a school—his starting point, not his destination. Even though it had the Blueberry League, that wasn't the same as a regional league.

"In that case, how about I make a recommendation?"

"Please do, Mr. Dylan."

"Go to the Alola region. You might just become Champion."

Dylan smiled at Kieran.

Yes, the Alola League was still new—and it needed a Champion-level trainer like Kieran.

---

---

---

# 313: Chapter 314: Lance the Dragon Master

"Alola Region?"

Kieran was stunned. He was from Kitakami, and if he truly wanted to grow, staying in Kitakami wasn't an option.

He had already reached the top of Blueberry Academy. Though he hadn't graduated yet, Kieran had no intention of staying there any longer.

Kitakami was simply too small. In truth, the region had only Mossui Town and a few scattered villages—not even a proper city.

(In the game, Mossui is the only town.)

So, if he really wanted to expand his future, he would have to go elsewhere.

Originally, Kieran had considered Unova or Paldea. In Unova, Alder's condition was well known—he was basically retired.

Kieran was already Champion-level. If he improved further next year, taking the Champion title in Unova wasn't out of reach.

As for Paldea, while Geeta, the Top Champion, was undeniably powerful, if Kieran could defeat her and the Elite Four, he would earn the Champion title there as well.

"Mr. Dylan, but… Alola doesn't even have a League, right?"

Carmine frowned. Her little brother was so strong now. As his sister, she was happy for him, though her feelings were complicated.

Still, she genuinely hoped for Kieran to have a bright future.

Alola was indeed famous worldwide—but for being a tourist paradise.

Without a Pokémon League, what was the point in going to Alola?

"Not quite! The application to establish the Alola League has already been approved. It's expected to be formally founded by the end of this year or early next," Lillie explained.

"Really?" The three of them were surprised.

"That's right. The approval came through a few days ago. Kieran, for a trainer, becoming a League Champion is the fastest way to make a name for yourself."

Dylan looked at Kieran seriously.

"Compared to the established leagues, Alola is still a blank slate. Everything has to start from scratch. That's exactly why now is the best time. If you go to Alola now, you'll have the greatest opportunity."

Kieran listened intently, his gaze flickering—he was tempted.

"You've got strength, talent, and most importantly, youth. Your future is wide open. Honestly, no matter which region you go to, I think you'll accomplish great things."

Dylan spoke highly of Kieran—deservedly so.

In the world of trainers, strength ruled. With Kieran's talent, another year of growth would put him among the best of the Champion class.

"But since the Alola League is just starting, if you go there now, the region's resources will be directed toward you. I'm sure you understand what that means," Dylan continued.

"What do you think, Sis?" Kieran hesitated and turned to Carmine. Since finding his resolve, he rarely sought her opinion—but that was mostly because he'd been so focused on intense training and raising his team.

"Well…" Carmine looked torn.

She agreed that Dylan wasn't wrong. Her brother had talent and youth. With his current strength, he could develop well in any region.

A newly established League meant that Alola's support would be poured into Kieran. That would help him grow even faster.

"I'll follow your lead, Kieran. If you want to go, I'll go with you!" Carmine finally said after a brief pause. She was sure their family would support Kieran's choice too.

"There's really nothing to hesitate about, right?" Lacey said, blinking. "I think you should definitely go. Who knows? You might become Alola's very first Champion."

To immediately become the Champion of a region's League—that would be a legendary accomplishment.

"But… won't the Alola League be kind of weak?" Carmine was still worried.

"Well, if Kieran doesn't go, maybe," Dylan chuckled. "But once he joins, it won't be."

"Professor Kukui of Alola is already Champion-level. I don't know if he intends to claim the Champion title himself, but he'll definitely support the League."

"And once you're there, Kieran, you can spar and learn from him. He's a great mentor—very open with his knowledge."

Dylan had full confidence in Kukui's character.

"Then… I'll do it, Mr. Dylan. I'll go to Alola!" Kieran said firmly, no longer hesitating.

He'd already been leaning toward that decision. So what if Alola was still weak? Once he grew stronger, Alola would become stronger too.

That was his pride as a prodigy.

"I think you'll be proud of this choice one day, Kieran. It's the right decision."

Talents like Kieran—unbound by any region—were always in high demand.

"Alola welcomes you~!" Lillie's eyes sparkled with joy, clearly thrilled for her homeland.

"Eh? Miss Lillie, you're from Alola?" Lacey and the others were surprised.

"Yep! I'm Alolan."

"But isn't Mr. Dylan your brother?" Carmine glanced between the two. Dylan was clearly from Kanto.

"Huh? Kanto's my home too. But Dylan isn't my biological brother—he's my honorary brother."

Lillie replied without hesitation, perfectly at ease.

Alola was her homeland, but Kanto was her home as well.

"I see." Carmine nodded. That explained it—Dylan and Lillie didn't really look alike anyway.

"Alola's a beautiful place, Kieran. When you get there, you can start by visiting Aether Paradise. I'll notify them in advance—Chairwoman Lusamine will make arrangements for you."

"Thank you," Kieran said gratefully.

"No need. Just focus on getting even stronger, Kieran. The Pokémon League will be hosting the World Championship next year—a global ranking tournament."

"I'll be looking forward to seeing your name high on that list."

Dylan took a sip of tea, his voice gentle yet filled with anticipation.

Who knew? Maybe Kieran could crack the top twenty. It wouldn't be easy.

Judging by the current outlook, next year would be full of monsters. For Kieran, reaching the top twenty would already be a huge success.

…

Indigo Plateau.

After being "missing" for several days, Lance finally returned—and the Kanto League's high council convened immediately.

Inside the conference room, Chairman Goodshow sat at the head. On his left were the League's councilors; on the right sat the Kanto Elite Four, led by Lance.

Lance sat with arms crossed, expression blank, staring at the table. No one could tell what he was thinking.

"The area near Lavender Town was completely destroyed. It'll take a long time to rebuild."

"This is outrageous! Lance, do you even care about the League anymore?!"

"You abandoned your post for this long without explanation. Is this how a Champion should act?"

"You said you went to eliminate Team Rocket, but you didn't bring back a single person? Arrogant! Do you even care about this League, Lance?!"

Several councilors vented their frustration. Normally they would've kept quiet, but this was their chance—they weren't about to hold back.

Lance had always been proud, cold, and intolerant of evil. He barely acknowledged their existence. To them, he was the biggest threat within the League's hierarchy.

At a certain word, Lance slowly looked up, his cold, emotionless gaze sweeping across the room.

The barking wouldn't stop. It was starting to annoy him.

His icy stare immediately silenced the bickering councilors. An eerie quiet fell over the room.

They were bold enough to attack him behind words—but not brave enough to face him directly.

Because he was Lance—the Dragon Master!

"Go on. Keep talking."

Lance stood slowly, his blood-red hair casting a shadow over his eyes. His entire presence radiated danger.

"Lance…" Goodshow's voice trembled slightly, trying to interject.

But Lance raised a hand to stop him. His boots echoed against the floor as he walked forward.

"I joined the League because I believed we could make the world better. There should be no place for sin or evil in a pure world."

Yes. This world should be clean. Pokémon, nature—they all deserve better.

"Then why… why are there always disgusting things—why do they always fester and rot everything they touch?!"

Lance's expression twisted, the sharp lines of his face warping into rage.

The room remained silent. Even the councilors sensed something was terribly wrong.

Lance's expression was terrifying—too terrifying.

"We've always tried to fight it…" Goodshow sighed wearily.

"But they never go away. The filthy maggots, the stench-ridden scum—they're right here!" Lance's eyes swept over the councilors.

He had always known some of them were rotten. The evidence he had reviewed made that clear.

Some had even conspired with Team Rocket.

"Evil is evil. Even dressed in human skin, their disgusting stench can't be hidden."

Murderous intent radiated from him.

Goodshow's pupils contracted. Lance…

"But now I know how to purge this rot once and for all. Chairman—watch carefully."

Lance's shadowed eyes gleamed with fury.

Behind him, a powerful Dragonite emerged. Its eyes mirrored Lance's own—dark, cold, dangerous.

A heavy, suffocating pressure filled the room—Dragonite's aura.

The councilors stared in horror at Lance and Dragonite. What was he about to do?

"Starting today, I, Lance the Dragon Master, resign from the Kanto League. I'll punish the darkness in my own way."

Lance's voice was devoid of emotion.

The League might strive toward light, but it was no longer a place for him. To Lance, it had become a shackle.

Goodshow shut his eyes in sorrow. So it had come to this, after all. Despite all his efforts—he couldn't stop it.

Agatha kept her eyes closed. Lorelei crossed her arms and smirked coldly at the councilors' pale faces.

As for Bruno, he simply lowered his head in silence, detached as always.

"Lance… is there truly no room for compromise?" Goodshow still tried to reach him.

"The League has changed," Lance said calmly.

"But before I go, let me do one last thing for it."

His cold eyes swept over the councilors—rats in human skin. This world didn't need such filth.

"I'll make this world clear and blue again!"

(End of Chapter)

---

---

---

# 314: Chapter 315: Let Dylan

"Dylan, are you free right now?"

"What's up, Chairman?"

"Come to Indigo Plateau. There's something important I need to discuss with you."

"Got it."

After hanging up, Dylan still found it strange—Chairman Goodshow's voice had sounded unusually grim.

Something serious must have happened.

Dylan had his sister watch over the gym, then flew toward Indigo Plateau on his white dragon.

The journey from Route Lota to Indigo Plateau wasn't far—about half an hour.

He was greeted by the same secretary as usual, but this time, the secretary's face was pale.

"Gym Leader Dylan, this way please. Chairman has been waiting for you."

She quickly led him inside the building.

Indigo Plateau—the most sacred site of the Kanto League. The League's main offices were located here.

But today, a heavy gloom hung over the place.

Inside the chairman's office sat Goodshow, Officer Jenny's Chief Commander, Nurse Joy's Chief, and three remaining council members—each of them somber.

The atmosphere was suffocating.

A knock on the door broke the silence. Goodshow snapped back to reality, his mind still replaying the earlier meeting.

Lance, Champion of Kanto, the revered Dragon Master idolized by all Kanto Trainers—had stood in a pool of blood, his cold, shadowed expression terrifying to behold.

"For a pure, blue tomorrow!"

That sentence still echoed in Goodshow's ears.

But… was that really the right way?

He closed his eyes tightly. The Kanto League had once had nine council members—now only three remained.

The other six… had all died under Lance's Dragonite's claws, their blood staining the meeting room.

Goodshow understood Lance's motive—eradicate evil completely—but in doing so, Lance himself had become the "evil" in the eyes of others.

"Chairman."

Dylan entered the room, quickly taking in the tense expressions before settling his gaze on Goodshow.

He was startled.

The chairman, normally energetic and optimistic despite his age, looked years older—drained and lifeless.

"Dylan… sit down."

Goodshow forced a smile, gesturing to the seat beside him.

Dylan raised a brow, glancing at Officer Jenny and Nurse Joy's leaders, and then at the remaining council members.

"I assume you're wondering why we called you so suddenly," Goodshow said, rising from his chair to sit beside Dylan, rubbing his forehead with weariness.

"Please speak openly, Chairman."

"I won't hide it from you. We've handled this matter strictly and won't let it leak—but the impact is unavoidable."

Goodshow's voice was heavy.

"Lance has left the Kanto League."

"…?"

Dylan blinked in shock.

Did Lance run off?

"And not just that—he went on a rampage in the meeting room. He killed six council members, including Fujii, before leaving… and…"

Goodshow sighed again.

"Agatha and the others went with him."

Dylan immediately understood—Lance had taken the entire Kanto Elite Four and gone rogue.

No, "gone rogue" wasn't quite right—they'd basically seceded from the League.

And true to his reputation, Lance didn't leave quietly—he massacred half the council on his way out.

"This is…"

Dylan furrowed his brow.

"Hmph, they got what they deserved. If they weren't so corrupt, Lance wouldn't have killed them," Councilman Sawamura scoffed angrily.

He didn't believe Lance had done wrong. Those rotten bureaucrats who abused their power—good riddance.

"Sawamura, mind your words," another councilman cautioned.

"Why should I?" Sawamura snapped. "I warned you what Fujii and the others were up to. Lance left because of people like them."

"And now look at us—Kanto doesn't even have an Elite Four anymore!"

His words plunged the room into silence again.

Kanto—the heart of the Pokémon League and headquarters of the global system—now had no Elite Four at all.

They had once considered splitting off Johto as its own league… but now? With what?

More than half the council had been wiped out. The League could barely function.

"We can't go on without an Elite Four. It's essential to Kanto's prestige," Goodshow said gravely. "Do any of you have candidates in mind?"

The League needed new strength. In the long run, Lance's purge might be a blessing—but for now, they were in deep trouble.

"The boys Red and Blue aren't bad. Their performance during the Saffron City incident was excellent," said the Jenny Commander.

"They're Oak's students, right? Promising indeed," Goodshow nodded.

Just one year into their journeys, they were already Champion-level—undeniable proof of talent.

But that was only two people.

They continued brainstorming. Trainers at Elite Four level did exist—like Sabrina of Saffron Gym.

But with legends like Lance and Agatha before them, replacements had to be truly strong.

"Isn't the right person already sitting here?" Councilman Sawamura suddenly asked, looking straight at Dylan.

"Gym Leader Dylan—and Gym Leader Giovanni of Viridian—they're both more than qualified. Dylan could easily be Champion."

Dylan glanced at him, amused. You sure know how to pick people.

"Dylan, what do you think?"

Goodshow knew Dylan was qualified—but also that he wasn't very interested in titles like Champion or Elite Four.

"Chairman, if Kanto needs me, I won't refuse. But you know my nature," Dylan said, a bit helpless.

He was happy to help fight evil for the League—but as Champion?

"I know this puts you in a tough spot. But Dylan, Kanto really needs you right now," Goodshow said with a heavy sigh.

All the Elite Four gone in a flash—Kanto's current situation was truly dire.

"You've always treated me with sincerity, Chairman. Of course I'll return the favor."

Dylan understood the weight of the request.

Goodshow had always treated him with honesty—refusing now would be too heartless.

And the League really was in a bind.

"If you don't mind, how about making you acting Champion?" suggested the Joy Commander. "Just until we fill the Elite Four and find a suitable permanent Champion. You wouldn't need to handle day-to-day duties."

They could all tell Dylan wasn't interested in the role itself.

But to gain the people's trust, and restore confidence, Kanto needed someone strong and respected.

Dylan had the highest reputation of any Gym Leader in the region. He was the perfect fit.

"…Fine."

Dylan looked at Goodshow's hopeful eyes and finally nodded.

Alright, alright—looks like he couldn't dodge this "Champion Trial Experience."

"Ha ha~!"

Goodshow finally relaxed. As long as they had Dylan, Kanto was still invincible!

Yes—Dylan alone could defeat the entire previous Elite Four!

"Now we just need two more."

"We already mentioned Giovanni," Dylan chuckled.

"Dylan—no, Champion Dylan—Giovanni is like you. He's never shown much interest in becoming an Elite Four," said the Jenny Commander.

Before Dylan came along, Giovanni had been Kanto's strongest Gym Leader.

During the Saffron City conflict, Giovanni had fought alongside Steven and Wallace to repel Zinnia, showing Champion-level power.

He'd been eligible to join the Elite Four for a long time—but never cared to.

"I know Giovanni well. Let me talk to him."

Dylan grinned.

Taking Giovanni along to challenge the now-rogue Lance and his former Elite Four? That would be… quite the sight.

"That just leaves one more."

"Why not Dylan's apprentice, Selene? I hear even Lorelei wasn't her match," said the Joy Commander.

"Selene is very strong," Dylan nodded. "But she's still a bit green—needs more polish."

"I do have a Champion-level friend I could ask. Not sure if she'd be interested, but I'll check."

He was thinking of Leaf—already Champion-tier, showcasing the prestige of her title.

Though carefree by nature, who knew if she'd want the job?

And if not, it was fine. Like him, she could be a temporary stand-in. By next year, Selene, Yellow, and Silver would be ready.

Then they could "retire gracefully."

"Perfect!" Goodshow beamed. All Champions! And Dylan as Champion himself?

Kanto would remain invincible!

"I'll have someone prep your official portrait. Dylan, what type should I list for your Champion profile—multi-type?"

Normally, a new Champion had to defeat all four Elite Four in a row. But with them all gone, the process was skipped.

Champion profiles on the League site included a preferred type. Dylan's was obviously multi-type, right?

"Type, huh?"

Dylan thought for a moment—then smiled.

"I'm Lance's successor, aren't I? Since I've taken his position, I'll inherit his type too."

"Mark me down as Dragon-type Champion."

(End of Chapter)

---

---

---

# 315: Chapter 316: I Want to Be Head

Champion's Chamber.

This was the highest room within the Kanto League building. Since Dylan had accepted the role of Kanto Champion—even if only a temporary stand-in—it still had to be officially recorded.

Goodshow led Dylan into the chamber. Before them stood a stone pedestal, and on the wall were portraits of previous Champions.

Dylan spotted both Professor Oak and Lance among them.

"Alright, Dylan. Place your ID card here," Goodshow said as he activated a control switch, revealing a stone console with a digital terminal embedded.

Dylan took out his Rotom Dex and inserted it into the port. His portrait appeared on the screen:

Black hair, sharp and clean-cut. His unzipped black leather jacket revealed a dark shirt beneath. A black cloak hung from his shoulders, and in his right hand, he held a deep purple Master Ball.

Yes—this was the photo Dylan had just taken for his Champion profile.

Name: DylanAge: 18Team: Ultra Necrozma, Kyurem, Reshiram, Dragonite, Salamence, Garchomp

Registration complete.

Goodshow stroked his beard with a curious look as he reviewed the team.

A full dragon-type squad. Clearly, Dylan hadn't been joking when he said he'd be a "Dragon-type Champion."

Now this was a proper Dragon team. Still… wasn't he making a certain statement with this lineup?

"All done. From this moment forward, Dylan, you are the Champion of Kanto," Goodshow said with a smile, hands clasped behind his back.

"Congratulations, Dylan."

"You flatter me, Chairman. But now that I'm officially Champion, wouldn't it be weird for me to still accept challengers at the Rota Gym?"

Dylan shrugged as he swished his new black Champion cloak.

Yeah—he had spontaneously decided on the cloak during his photo shoot. A Dragon-type Champion needed one, after all. Just look at Lance and Zinnia—capes were a must.

"No one will think it's odd. You're not using your main team to face challengers anyway," Goodshow said casually.

After all, they had already agreed Dylan wouldn't be burdened with full Champion duties.

Though this was the first time in history that a region's Champion also served as a Gym Leader, it wasn't a big deal. Gym challenges and Champion challenges were two different things. Anyone who thought they had what it took to beat Dylan at full power could try.

Frankly, Goodshow was pleased. If someone managed to beat Dylan's real team, they could take over as Champion, and Dylan could go back to running his gym. Everybody wins.

"I'll go contact Giovanni and my friend now."

"Great. I need to reach out to Professor Oak too."

Their tasks set, Dylan and Goodshow parted ways. Goodshow was going to ask Red and Blue to join the Elite Four. Dylan had his own calls to make.

After flying back to Rota Gym on Reshiram, he saw Leaf and Silver sparring in the training grounds—or rather, Leaf was clearly coaching Silver.

"Huh? Big bro, when did you start wearing a cape?" Lillie ran over curiously, eyeing the cloak. Gotta say—it looked pretty cool on him.

Wait—he always looked cool!

"Not bad, right? From now on, your big brother is Kanto's Champion," Dylan said with a cheeky grin, giving her cheek a playful squeeze.

"Whaaat?" Lillie's eyes widened into perfect circles—adorably stunned.

"Wait—Gym Leader Dylan… you're the Champion now?" Asha and Yellow both looked equally shocked.

What the heck? He'd only been gone a few hours and now he was Champion?

"Yep. It's a bit complicated, but I'm officially Champion now. The public announcement should go out once the rest of the Elite Four are finalized," Dylan explained.

"Elite Four finalized…?" Lillie and the others blinked in confusion.

Meanwhile, Leaf and Silver wrapped up their training match. Leaf strolled over.

"Hey~ Dylan, this cape looks awesome on you."

She tugged at the black fabric, admiring the style.

"Not bad, huh? Anyway, Leaf—we need a new Elite Four. Want to be one?"

"Ehhh… since it's you asking, sure. Why not?"

Leaf put her hands on her hips, smiling. She didn't refuse—her bond with Dylan ran deep.

She had no grand ambitions anymore. Thanks to Dylan, she'd found her parents. These days, she enjoyed working as a referee at the gym. Once the Masked Organization was dealt with, she could finally relax.

If she had to name one goal now…

Leaf's gaze lingered on Dylan's face. Yep—that was it.

"So, who else is joining the Elite Four?" she asked, sitting down beside him.

"Red and Blue. As for the last member—let me call him now."

Dylan took out his phone and made the call.

It didn't take long to connect.

"Dylan," came a low, magnetic voice.

Leaf's eyes widened in recognition. Wait—is he serious?

"Giovanni, you've heard what happened with Lance, right?" Dylan said lightly.

"Heh… I didn't expect it to go so smoothly. That old rat Fujii really came through," Giovanni chuckled darkly.

There had always been a councilman in contact with Team Rocket—Fujii. Giovanni had helped orchestrate a showdown between him and Lance at the meeting, pushing Lance into extremism and alienating him from the League.

Now Lance was outside the system, free of its restraints—his actions would only grow more radical. Sooner or later, he'd clash with the League again.

"Kanto's in disarray. Even I got roped into being Champion," Dylan said.

"Congratulations, Dylan," Giovanni said with a low laugh.

"But we still need a full Elite Four. Giovanni—how about you take one of the seats?"

The line went silent.

Dylan waited patiently.

Giovanni, on the other hand, was dumbfounded.

You want me… to be part of the Kanto Elite Four???

"Are you serious?"

"Absolutely. With your strength, you could be Champion," Dylan replied calmly.

Giovanni didn't disagree. He had the strength—but no interest in titles.

"Dylan… I'm the leader of Team Rocket."

If he ever wanted a League title, he could've claimed it long ago. Even Lance didn't scare him.

"In name only. Rocket is gone."

"The League won't accept me."

"They will—because I'm recommending you."

"…Giovanni's name is just a legend now. The real Rocket boss was 'Beragon,' remember?" Dylan added slyly.

That's why, during the Saffron conflict, Giovanni's identity had remained hidden. The Rocket executive Petrel had even impersonated "Giovanni" in battle.

No one would ever connect the real Giovanni with Rocket now. He was still the respected Gym Leader of Viridian.

"I've got important things to do, Dylan."

Giovanni was trying to find his son—he didn't have time for titles and politics.

"I don't know what you're chasing, but becoming Elite Four gives you access to League resources. That could help."

Giovanni frowned on the other end of the line. That… hit the mark.

He'd been searching for Silver for years with no leads. But League intel? That could change everything.

"Kanto's evil groups are mostly gone now. Besides…"

Dylan leaned in.

"Don't you want this, Giovanni?"

"Lance has gone off the deep end. He's broken from the League and thinks he can create a 'pure' world. But with his methods… Kanto's in for chaos."

"When the League inevitably clashes with him, it'll fall to us—the new Elite Four—to stand against him."

"Your position is already the opposite of Lance's."

Leaf's eyes widened. Holy crap…

Lance had always hunted Team Rocket relentlessly. Now, he was the one in darkness. If Giovanni joined the League…

That's a complete reversal!

The man Lance once called the greatest evil might now be the one to stop him.

The silence dragged on.

"…Heh heh heh… Dylan, you really are something."

"So what do you say, Giovanni?"

"…Fine. You've convinced me."

"Haha! You won't regret it."

Dylan was pleased. Getting Giovanni onboard was going to make things very interesting.

"But Dylan—I want to be Head of the Elite Four," Giovanni added coolly.

The Champion seat was taken, but he wasn't going to be anyone's subordinate.

Even without his ace Beedrill—still tied to the identity of "Beragon"—his ground-type lineup alone was terrifying.

Giovanni had thought it through. Becoming Elite Four came with no downsides.

He'd get access to the League's intelligence, helping him search for his son—and he'd have an official excuse to oppose Lance, that mad dog.

"The scourge of Kanto. The evil tyrant Lance…"

Just imagining himself pointing at Lance and calling him that made Giovanni's lips curl into a smirk.

This… would be very satisfying indeed.

---

# 316: Chapter 317: Blue vs. Giovanni?

"You're kidding, Dylan. You really made Giovanni one of the Kanto Elite Four?"

Leaf was completely stunned.

Giovanni was the former leader of Team Rocket—Kanto's greatest enemy. And now he was going to be one of the Elite Four?Had the world gone mad?

"A Giovanni without ambition is reliable. And if that ambition returns, there are people who can keep him in check."

Dylan shrugged. In Black and White 2, Giovanni had even participated in the World Championship. Becoming an Elite Four member? No big deal.

Besides, Giovanni's main goal right now wasn't domination—it was finding Silver. And when the day came that his ambition reignited, people like Red and Selene would be strong enough to keep him grounded.

"Whatever. I guess if anyone can handle him, it's you," Leaf muttered. If Dylan had made the call, she wasn't going to worry too much. He always had his reasons.

"Chairman, it's all arranged," Dylan informed Goodshow over the phone.

"Excellent! Dylan, let's schedule everything for tomorrow at Indigo Plateau. The four of you can be officially sworn in."

Goodshow sounded relieved. His end had gone just as smoothly. After hearing the full story, Professor Oak had immediately contacted Red and Blue, who accepted without hesitation.

They'd been preparing to challenge the League anyway. Being appointed straight to the Elite Four was even better.

"But Oak mentioned his grandson is fine with you being Champion," Goodshow added, "but he's dead set on claiming the title of head of the Elite Four."

"Typical Blue," Dylan said with a chuckle.

If Dylan hadn't taken the Champion seat, Blue might have challenged it himself.

"As for the head Elite Four seat… Giovanni's already called dibs. If Blue wants it, he'll have to battle Giovanni for it."

Rankings were earned, not given. If Blue wanted to be first, he'd have to prove it in battle.

As for last place… well, that was obviously Leaf.

"Perfect. I'll pass that along. Let's meet tomorrow afternoon?"

"No problem, Chairman," Dylan agreed.

The newly appointed Kanto Elite Four were about to gather for the first time. Giovanni would have to come from Johto, after all.

"Very well. I'll await you at Indigo Plateau." Goodshow seemed to be in good spirits again.

Despite the pain of Lance and the others "defecting," it felt like a new, solid foundation was forming.

With Dylan at the helm, it couldn't be anything but stronger than before.

"Thank Arceus for Kanto's deep bench of talent," he murmured.

Goodshow walked to the window of his office and looked out over the Indigo Plateau with a sigh.

Without this regional talent pool, the sudden loss of all four Elite Four members would have been devastating—maybe even a fatal blow to Kanto's prestige.

"The Gym Leader seats can't stay empty either," he muttered and began heading toward the cells where Janine and others were being held.

Back at Rota Gym...

"You're raising another new set of main Pokémon? You're seriously a monster."

Leaf stared at the three Poké Balls Dylan had just retrieved from the transporter.

"What? I told you—I'm building a full-type gym. I need all attributes to be top tier." Dylan rolled his eyes.

"Yeah, yeah, total dominance in every type. You're nuts."

Rota Gym was already the stuff of legend. Dylan had mastered Poison, Dark, Ghost, Dragon, Grass, Fire, Normal, Water, and Ice—plus Gold-tier legendaries. And every single Pokémon was absurdly strong.

Leaf couldn't even begin to wrap her head around how he trained them all.

She was supposed to be a genius too, but Dylan made her feel like a total novice.

"Who knows? Maybe I'll actually pull it off," Dylan said with a smile.

"So what's the type this time?" Leaf asked, intrigued.

"Come out."

Dylan released the three Pokémon.

The first was a humanoid, doll-like Pokémon with a purple and white body. It had a soft smile and droopy twin-horn "pigtails." Red triangular markings framed its eyes.

Indeed—it was Indeedee.

Bzzt~ Indeedee (female), Psychic/Normal type, Emotion PokémonAbility: Psychic SurgeMoves: Psybeam, Disarming Voice, Baton Pass, Helping Hand, Stored Power, Fake Out (inherited)

It seemed this batch would be Psychic types.

Leaf studied the gentle-looking Indeedee. Dylan already had Normal-type coverage, so this one was here for the Psychic angle.

Next was a tiny creature with soft pink skin, a little mouth, and stumpy limbs. Its pale blue hair curled inward to cover its eyes, and its tail stood upright like a party hat.

So cute.

Bzzt~ Hatenna, Psychic type, Calm PokémonAbility: Magic BounceMoves: Aromatic Mist, Disarming Voice, Life Dew, Confusion, Play Nice (inherited)

"Hatenna, huh? I think that's the female counterpart to Impidimp?"

Leaf recalled that Dylan had a Grimmsnarl on his team already.

"Exactly," Dylan confirmed.

Some Pokémon came in gendered evolutionary lines—Hatenna's final form, Hatterene, was the counterpart to Grimmsnarl. A forest witch versus a fairy demon.

Then Leaf turned to the last one: a very quiet little figure.

Small, humanoid, white body. Its pale blue hair covered most of its face, with yellow horns protruding from the head.

It was a shiny Ralts.

Bzzt~ Ralts, Psychic/Fairy type, Feeling PokémonAbility: TraceMoves: Draining Kiss, Hypnosis, Confusion, Double Team, Mean Look (inherited)

"Whoa! That's a shiny Ralts!"

Leaf blinked. Regular Ralts had green hair—this one was clearly blue.

"Yep. My dad, Steven, and Wallace helped me find it. Took some effort, but we finally tracked one down."

Dylan grinned. Ralts was a Hoenn-native species, and Steven and Wallace—former and current Hoenn Champions—had the resources to search thoroughly.

Most importantly, this shiny Ralts was female.

Some people believed Gallade was Ralts' "true" male evolution, but male Ralts could still evolve into Gardevoir too.

Dylan didn't care. He wanted that shiny Gardevoir aesthetic.

He appreciated strength, sure—but aesthetics mattered too. And a male Gardevoir just felt… off.

He'd considered Gallade if this one had been male—it was strong enough.

But a male Gardevoir? That was a hard no. Just too weird.

"Hello there. I'm Dylan. From now on, I'll be your Trainer."

He crouched and smiled warmly. The power of his Viridian Force spilled forth.

The sacred aura immediately endeared him to Indeedee and Hatenna, who leaned in close to nuzzle him. Even the shy shiny Ralts raised her head and stepped forward.

"Let's get stronger together," Dylan said, patting each of their heads.

With this, his Psychic team was nearly complete.

What a wonderful day.

The next afternoon…

Dylan flew to Indigo Plateau with Leaf and Lillie, leaving Lillie to wait outside while he and Leaf entered Goodshow's office.

Inside, Red, Blue, and Giovanni were already present.

"Sorry, looks like we're a bit late," Dylan said with a smile, greeting each of them.

"No trouble at all. Take a seat. You must be Leaf?" Goodshow asked kindly.

"Yes, sir. It's an honor to meet you, Chairman Goodshow," Leaf said politely, bowing slightly.

Red: "…"

Red tilted his head. When did Leaf get so formal?

"What a fine young lady. Please, sit."

Goodshow was clearly charmed. She was beautiful, talented, and well-mannered—a perfect candidate.

"You all know the situation. If you're here, that means you've agreed to be the new Kanto Elite Four."

"Once you fill out this paperwork and sign, you'll be official," Goodshow said, sliding the forms forward.

Giovanni picked his up and read carefully. It was mostly about resources, responsibilities, and rules. Standard stuff.

The League was fairly lenient overall. Even though the Elite Four were a region's frontline force, their titles were always subject to change.

"I've got one question," Blue said, leaning back in his chair. "Where are the old Four?"

He respected the previous Elite Four—especially Lance, the former Champion. Oak had only told him they'd stepped down, but not why.

"Don't know," Goodshow sighed. "I can't tell you where they went. But I can tell you this—someday, they might become your enemies."

His voice was heavy with regret.

"They've turned their backs on the League."

Maybe if he'd acted sooner, Lance wouldn't have gone to extremes. But hindsight was useless now.

Lance had carried too much darkness for too long. Now that he was free from League oversight, his path was bound to diverge further.

The League stood for justice. Lance, who had killed council members, clearly no longer shared those ideals.

"Huh." Blue raised a brow. That was a lot to process.

The former Elite Four might be their future opponents?

"Sounds fun." He grinned. He didn't care about their reasons. If they were enemies, they were enemies.

Losing? Please. When did the current beat the former?

And besides—they had Dylan now!

Dylan, his invincible best bro!

"One more thing—I've got no issue with Dylan being Champion. But who's going to be the head of the Elite Four?" Blue asked, arms crossed as he glanced around.

Dylan was Champion, fine. But the #2 seat—Elite Four head—was his to claim!

There could be only one second-in-command: Blue!

"I'm not fighting for it. I'll take the bottom spot," Leaf said immediately.

What was there to fight for? She wasn't beating any of these guys anyway.

And besides—wasn't it nice being the cute little sister with four big brothers?

"The order's up to you," Goodshow said. "You can battle to settle it."

Red pulled down his hat, eyes narrowing as he stared back at Blue. He wasn't about to give up the top spot.

"Well then, I'll say it now—the top spot's mine. Anyone object?"

Blue stood up, full of swagger.

Giovanni? Been there, beat that.Red? Same deal.

This time, the top seat belonged to him!

"Let's battle and find out," Giovanni said with a cool smile.

He had his pride. Now that he'd joined, he wasn't about to let some punk stand above him.

"Uncle Gio, you already lost to us. You really want to go again?" Blue scoffed.

"Gym battles are gym battles," Giovanni replied smoothly, sipping his tea with practiced grace.

"Tch. You saying you didn't go all out back then?"

Leaf: (°—°)Red: "…"

"Heh… Blue, let Uncle show you the true meaning of the earth," Giovanni said with a smirk.

These kids needed their egos trimmed.
